# World block (b8-world): staged implementation specification

> **Note.** This is the implementation specification the world block was built from, kept as written. It cites working notes, pre-flight and review reports, and run records that are not part of this release (marked "not published" or cited by short name); the numbers a decision rests on are quoted in the text, and `scripts/world/` and `scripts/world_check.py` regenerate the gates. Commit hashes name commits of the development history, which is not published. "The lead modeller's tree" is the research working folder; its published parts are listed in section 1.1.

**For:** careful junior Rust engineers (one stage per branch, one independent review between stages).
**Branches:** `b8-world-s1` … `b8-world-s5`, each from the previous stage's reviewed head; stage 1 from master after joint calibration 3 (call that commit `M3`). **Worktrees:** one git worktree per branch.
**Research inputs (read-only):** `data/world/research/{blocs,trade,oil,finance}/params.json` and the CSV files beside them (published under `data/`); the lead modeller's prototypes and golden numbers are published as the reference tooling in `scripts/world/` (`paths.py`, `pstar.json`, `targets.json`, `world_ref.py` and its siblings) and the goldens in `crates/econsim-core/src/world/data/golden/` (the lead modeller's working copies of `param_map` and `config_keys` are not published; the oil-market research prototype is published as `scripts/world/oil_reference.py`). The reference tooling regenerates every golden from the committed defaults with no hidden overrides: `python3 -B scripts/world/world_ref.py selftest | gates | w109 | kcheck | goldens --write|--check | gen-inputs clean|stress`; the placement table of section 1.1 is verified by running `selftest` and `goldens --check` in the target layout (`scripts/world/make_layout.py DEST`).
**Rules that bind every stage:** the b7 spec conventions (the batch-7 tariff specification, not published, sections 11 and 13: new code off by default with a profile; `// b8-world` blocks in `Config`/`Default`; legacy semantics when off; the 8-process limit, `RAYON_NUM_THREADS=1`, `python3 -B`; never `--no-verify`; every commit message ends with `Co-Authored-By: Claude Haiku 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`). **Never retype a research number:** every coefficient below is cited by a `params.json` path and is copied by `scripts/world/build_world_params.py` (section 1.7). Anchors are text anchors (function names, comment markers), not line numbers, because b7 and joint calibration 3 move lines.

**Status flags used below.** *[assumed]* = my design assumption, no source. *[derived]* = computed from cited numbers by the stated formula. *[verify@M3]* = a master symbol that must be re-read after the batch-7 merge. *[U]* = unverified in the research. *[verify@stage1]* = a number measured on joint2-era runs that stage 1 must re-measure on the `M3` binary.
**Revisions 2 and 3** (after the adversarial review and its verification): every change is listed in the final sections "Revision 2 change log" and "Revision 3 change log"; decisions D28-D36 record the rulings. **Revision 7** (stage-4 pre-flight against `ccdcc48`, 2026-10-12): decisions D37-D42, section 1.6b, the rewritten sections 2.6-2.8 and Stage 4, the protocol paragraph of 3.0, rows W4-29/W4-31/W4-32 and the "Revision 7 change log"; the evidence is in the stage 4 pre-flight note (not published). **Revision 8** (sub-round 4-2 pre-flight against the list-A head of `b8-world-s4a`, 2026-10-12): decisions D43-D46, the official part of section 2.7, hook H5 (`rebalance_row`), the finance shocks and metrics, the 1.6b rows of the official block, the sub-round 4-2 text, the 4-2 protocol paragraph of 3.0, rows W4-04 (10y half), W4-10..W4-14, W4-20, W4-32 (4-2 prefix), new rows W4-33 and W4-34, and the "Revision 8 change log"; the evidence is in the stage 4-2 pre-flight note (not published). **Revision 9** (list B, sub-round 4-1b: the dollar-link redesign, against `cc33f15`, 2026-10-13): decisions D47-D49, the forecast reference of sections 1.6b, 2.5 and 2.6, the bound of 2.2.2, the 4-1b protocol paragraph of 3.0 and the sub-round 4-1b text, rows W4-28, W4-29, W4-31 and W4-32 restated and new rows W4-35 and W4-36, section 7 items 17 (resolved) and 28-31, and the "Revision 9 change log"; the evidence is in the list-B pre-flight note (not published). **Revision 10** (sub-round 4-3 pre-flight against `cc33f15`, 2026-10-13): decisions D51-D54, hooks H19-H22, the keys and metrics of retaliation and positions, the 1.6b rows of both, the retaliation paragraph of 2.5, section 2.8 rewritten, the sub-round 4-3 text, the 4-3 protocol paragraph of 3.0, rows W4-21..W4-25, W4-27, W4-32 (4-3 prefix), new rows W4-37 and W4-38, and the "Revision 10 change log"; the evidence is in the stage 4-3 pre-flight note (not published). **Revision 11** (stage-5 pre-flight against `d5aa81d`, 2026-10-13): decisions D55-D61 (the ladder and its classes, the re-specified rows, the centring constants of the coupled import-demand term, the profile family and `legacy_b8off.json`, the oil early gates, the start-state rule, the joint default switch), identities I5-I9, the stage-5 protocol paragraph of 3.0, the Stage 5 text rewritten, rows W1-31, W2-06, W2-10, W3-14, W4-07, W4-08, W4-26 and W5-01..W5-05 restated and new rows W4-39 and W5-06..W5-09, the presets of section 5 with their needs and two new site presets, section 7 items 36-43, and the "Revision 11 change log"; the evidence is in the stage 5 pre-flight note (not published). **Revision 12** (stage-5 review and the sub-round 5-T2 pre-flight against `212af62`, 2026-10-13): decisions D62-D64 (the drift of the block's import price against PT, the uniform-duty fold, the 5-T2 protocol), the re-measured centring constants of D57, the key `world_import_rel_drift`, the import-price paragraph of 2.5, the 5-T2 protocol paragraph of 3.0 and the sub-round 5-T2 text, identity I10, rows W2-15 (provenance note, band unchanged), W2-18 (new, R) and W5-06 (restated, E), section 7 items 44-46 and the "Revision 12 change log"; the evidence is in the stage 5 review note (not published) and the 5-T2 pre-flight note (not published).

---

## 0. Summary and decisions

### 0.1 What is built

A world block of 12 semi-structural bloc models (GPM form, monthly, rational expectations solved offline, 33 states each) plus one endogenous oil market, linked to the unchanged agent-based US model (ABM) through bilateral trade by sector, origin-specific import prices with dollar invoicing, bilateral and broad dollar rates, foreign Treasury demand in the term premium, a global financial factor tied to `f_t`, tariff retaliation and memo cross-border positions that reconcile exactly to the ledger. `world_block_enabled` is `false` by default. With it off, the output is byte-identical to `M3` (the new `Metrics.world` key is not serialised when absent). The block is built in five stages; each stage's link has its own switch, so a failing stage ships off without blocking the others.

### 0.2 Decisions (each research open question)

| # | Question | Decision | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Financial channel and the sign of foreign output after a US tightening (blocs REPORT 4.4) | Keep the three reduced-form terms of `blocs` `global.financial_channel`: a persistent state `G_t` (US policy-rate gap, monthly persistence 0.9283, lag 3 months) that lowers demand (`theta`) and raises the UIP premium (`lambda_rp`), and rule pass-through `k_us`. Pre-registered starting values **P\*** (table 2.2.4): `theta` x0.8 for AE, IN, KR, BR, MX, RoW, OPEC+; `lambda_rp` 1.5 (AE), 2.0 (floaters, RoW, OPEC+), 0.3 (CN); `k_us` 0.3 (JP 0.1) for AE, 0.4 for floaters, CN 0.2, RoW 0.33, OPEC+ 0.325; new fear-of-floating term `g_z = 0.25` [assumed] in the rule of IN, KR, BR, MX, RoW; CN `uip_w` 0.85. In stage 4 the risk factor `F_t` is a second, transient driver (D21). | Without it the GPM channels give +0.10 at h4 for AE (wrong sign). With P\* the reference simulation gives an AE5 trough of -0.44 and EME5 -0.53 (W1-10, W1-11). Calibrated on figure reads of one panel (Iacoviello-Navarro 2019), so bands are troughs, not horizon points. |
| D2 | AE policy-rate responses 2-4x too large; emerging ones wrong-signed | Not fixed by retuning the rule. (a) `k_us` AE 0.3 and a fear-of-floating term for floaters make the floaters' rate rise (+0.64 peak, W1-15). (b) AE rate band widened to the evidence range: Miranda-Agrippino-Rey about -0.3, Kalemli-Ozcan implied about -0.9, Degasperi et al. +0.3 on impact (W1-13, W1-14). Model: +0.14 at m3, -0.94 at m24. (c) The residual (3x Miranda-Agrippino-Rey) is recorded as an open item. (d) The ELB is not in the dynamics (rates are reported floored). | The "2-4x" is relative to one study; the three studies disagree by 3x. The AE easing comes from disinflation times a long-run inflation coefficient of 1.9-2.3 (blocs REPORT 4.4), a property of the published GPM rule. |
| D3 | Emerging currencies depreciate 5-8x too little | The 15.7% (Kalemli-Ozcan 2019, eight quarters) is the unconditional upper bound; the paper says it fades within about four quarters once domestic policy is controlled. Gate on finance V06 (+8%, range 2-16%). Stage 1 (bloc UIP alone): floaters +4.2% peak, +3.1% at m24 (W1-18, W1-19). Stage 4 adds the risk channel (13 VIX points per 100bp x 0.15-0.30%/point = +2-4% on impact) so the total reaches 6-8% in year one (W4-08). | The closed-form UIP with `phi` 0.8 cannot produce a persistent premium; the empirical dollar-VIX channel can, and it is independently estimated (finance 4.2). |
| D4 | Oil supply share of price variance: 9% (default) vs 30-45% (Baumeister-Hamilton, Caldara et al.) | Default `world_oil_profile = "default"` (supply 0.09, oil-specific 0.66, activity 0.25). `"supply_heavy"` (oil `alternatives.supply_heavy`: 0.35 supply) is run as a sensitivity in stage 5 on W3-05, W3-06, W3-15. Switch the default only if it passes W3-01..W3-04 at least as well and improves every row of W3-05/W3-15. | The impulse responses are anchored to Kilian; the price effect per lost barrel (2.8-3.2 log points per 1% of supply, within a year) is mid-range for Baumeister-Hamilton (3.2-4.7), so supply-cut presets are right either way. Only the composition of baseline noise differs. |
| D5 | Oil price persistence shortfall (level AR(1) 0.960 vs 0.978; 20-year level sd 0.32 vs 0.44) and the activity process (the prototype's activity AR(1) has a level sd of 15.6%; bloc and ABM gaps have 1-2%) | **No extra activity-noise term in the integrated run**: the bloc activity is the activity. The prototype activity (`shocks.activity.{sigma, rho_y}` = 1.6657, 0.9943) is used only by the isolated `oil-check` market (rows W3-02, W3-05, W3-06, W3-09; keys `world_oilcheck_activity_*`), drawn on `WORLD_OIL_S`. W3-05/W3-06 are the isolated rows (F); the integrated rows are W3-15 (G, data bands) and W3-19 (R). **Expected integrated `oil_lvl` is 0.27, at the floor of the band 0.27-0.52** (`scripts/world/oil_activity.py`: 0.269 with activity sd 2% or none; prototype activity 0.335). Pre-authorised levers, in this order: `world_oil_rho_q` 0.99 -> 0.995 (0.28), `world_oil_sigI_mult` up to 1.1 (0.29, vol 10.1), both (0.305). | The shortfall is inside the fact band; closing it needs implausible stock builds (oil REPORT 9.2). The activity gap of the blocs is far smaller than the prototype's. |
| D6 | Stochastic-mean offset (8.355 log points) | Subtract `world_oil_mean_offset` (default 8.355, `anchors.stochastic_mean_offset`) from the market log price **only when** `stochastic_oil` is true, at the ABM interface and in `rfx`/bloc oil gaps; never in deterministic runs. Gate W3-14. | Cuts are unbounded, increases are capped by spare capacity, so the stochastic mean sits above the zero-shock anchor (oil REPORT 9.3). |
| D7 | OPEC+ after the reported 2026 UAE exit (secondary source) | Membership is data, not code. All tables are built for the 21-member composition used by all four researchers (UAE inside). `world_uae_in_opec` (default `true`) switches the **GDP weight** (`blocs` `global.composition_notes.composites.opecplus.ex_UAE_oct2026`) and the **oil** quantities and spare capacity (UAE share from `oil/data/eia_intl_annual_liquids_1990_2025.csv`, spare share `opec_uae_spare_share` 0.30 [assumed]) in one place (both composition sets are embedded in `world_params.json` as `opecplus.uae_in` and `opecplus.uae_out`, so the flag is a runtime switch and needs no regeneration). Trade and TIC tables keep the UAE inside OPEC+ (second-order for the US; recorded). Test `uae_flag_conserves_world_gdp`. | If the exit is confirmed, a one-line default flip makes the spec correct; if not, nothing changes. All 2024 data (EIA, BIMTS, TIC) are for the 21-member bloc. |
| D8 | Dollar after tariffs (+7.0% in 2018-19, -6.7% in 2025) | No hard-wired sign. The sign emerges from the Taylor rule (b7: policy +56bp for 10%), bloc UIP and the risk term. A new `UsCredibility{tp_bp, months, dollar_pct}` shock (D31: an explicit dollar-premium term plus a term-premium shock) reproduces the April 2025 regime (dollar -3.3%, 10y +22bp) by construction. The tariff-war preset is run with and without it; sign is reported, size gated (/dollar/ <= 8%) (W4-25). | Two opposite episodes; the model must be able to produce both. |
| D9 | Retaliation rule | Adopt `retaliation.recommended_rule` (trade params): `tau*_{j,s} = min(cap_j, kappa_j w_s max_{k<=lag_j} tau^{US->j}_{t-k})`, ratchet `mu_up 1`, `mu_down 0.1`; keys `EU`->`EA`; `IN` lag 12 (the JSON; the report omits it). Export response: value elasticity on the duty-inclusive price 1.0 impact, 3.0 long run, speed 0.10 [assumed, calibrated to W4-24: -2.4 per ln(1+tau*) at 12 months, inside the 2025 China check -2.0..-3.5]. FGKK 1.04 is the sectoral short-run lower bound. Retaliation acts only on `Tariff` shocks flagged `retaliate`. Rev 10 restates the rule in D51 (the lag is a delay, the weights, the neutrality). | Evidence: 2018-19 retaliation and the 2025 China ratchet (31.9% persisting). |
| D10 | Master constants the block supersedes (when on) | `r_world` (fx.rs, fixed) -> bloc real rates; bloc price levels are reported only (`common::world_price` / `world_goods_inflation` are read by `fx.rs`, `goods.rs` (three sites) and `metrics.rs` and are **not** changed); export driver (last month's nominal US GDP, `export_base_share_gdp`, `export_elasticity_*`, `export_lag_speed`) -> foreign income with the fiscal trend GDP and trade-matrix base shares; `import_pass_through_*` -> dominant-currency states; oil AR(1)/GARCH/jump process (`oil_ar`, `oil_sigma`, `oil_t_nu`, `oil_garch_*`, `oil_jump_*`) -> the market. **Kept:** `import_share`, `firm_*_import_share`, `import_price_elasticity`/`import_elasticity_lr`/`import_lag_speed` (the aggregate import-vs-domestic nest; see D13), all b7 `tariff_*` keys, `fx_psi_*` (US-side dollar premium), the `Dollar`/`OilPrice`/`ImportTariff` shocks, `oil_base`, `oil_index_to_world`, the fuel line, extraction labour, `auction_shares`. | Section 1.8 tables the on/off mapping. |
| D11 | Nominal-rate identity in the blocs report (E9) | Use the derived identity `ln S = z/100 + ln P_j - ln PT_us` (FC per USD); report E9 has the sign of the inflation differential reversed. Unit test `nominal_rate_depreciates_with_higher_foreign_inflation`. | With `z = ln S + ln P_us - ln P_j`, higher foreign inflation at constant `z` must depreciate the foreign currency. |
| D12 | Oil per-bloc elasticities | The bloc table `blocs.*.sr_crude_price_elasticity` aggregates to 0.050, not the aggregate `demand_aggregate.eta0` 0.100 (it embeds the `low_demand_elasticity` alternative). **Do not copy the bloc price-elasticity columns** (the activity elasticity `beta_i` = `blocs.<n>.cyclical_income_elasticity`, consumption-weighted 0.85, is a different column and is used). Compute `eta0_i = eta0 * phi_i / phibar` and `eta1_i = eta1 * phi_i / phibar` in code with `phibar = sum_i omega_i phi_i`, so the aggregates are exactly 0.100 and 0.2001. Test `oil_bloc_elasticities_aggregate`. | Internal inconsistency in the oil deliverable. |
| D13 | Master import elasticities (0.2/0.6) vs research (0.7/1.3) | Keep master's in stage 2; if W2-01 fails, step `(import_price_elasticity, import_elasticity_lr)` through `(0.4, 1.0)` then `(0.7, 1.3)` (the smallest that passes, no-harm rule of section 3.0). **The change rides in the world profile `scripts/profiles/b8-world-s2.json`, never in the `Config` defaults**, so I1 is untouched; stage 5's `legacy_b8off.json` resets both keys. Hand calculation of real imports for `Dollar +10%` (share factor only, `scripts/world/trade_hand.py`): master pair +1.6 / +2.3 / +2.7% at 12 / 24 / 36 months (the 24-month value is just below the lower edge 2.4 of W2-01), `(0.4, 1.0)` +2.9 / +4.0 / +4.6, `(0.7, 1.3)` +4.0 / +5.4 / +6.1. If the fallback fires, W2-01 is a fit check and is reported as F for that run. | The change alters US behaviour beyond the block; make it only if the gate demands it. |
| D14 | Export driver and base shares | `X_{s,j} = x0_{s,j} * fiscal::trend_gdp_month(w) * income * price * retaliation`. Base shares `x0` from `sector_shares.matrices.exports_base_C26_in_Tech` over the 2024 GDP (sum 10.96% of GDP), replacing master's `export_base_share_gdp` ([1.2, 0.8, 3.5, 2.0, 1.2, 0.8, 0.2, 1.3, 0.0]% of GDP over Energy ... Construction, sum 11.0%). The new shares are Energy 1.16, Food 0.64, Durables 3.03, Nondurables 1.43, Tech 1.26, Leisure 0.69, HealthEdu 0.20, OtherServices 2.53, Construction 0.01% (largest changes: OtherServices +1.2 pp, Nondurables -0.6, Durables -0.5). | Removes the feedback loop from the US cycle to exports through nominal GDP (b7-spikes finding); data-based sector mix. |
| D15 | Equilibrium real-exchange-rate shocks (GPM random walk) | Not in the bloc gaps. Bilateral FX noise = stationary AR(1) disconnect noise `zeta_j` (rho 0.99, sd 1.5% AE / 2.0% EME per month) added to `ln S_j` in stage 4 only; blocs do not feel it. | A random walk makes bilateral rates drift 50% in 20 years; AR(1) 0.99 matches the existing `fx_psi` treatment. |
| D16 | Neutral-rate and potential-growth shocks; the ELB | Off (fixed steady states); ELB not in dynamics. | Needs RE-consistent state changes the offline solution cannot carry; downside scenarios stay above the floors (EA neutral nominal 2.1% vs floor -0.5%). |
| D17 | Expectations of exogenous inputs | The US policy-rate path is the ABM's own expected path (new helper `monetary::expected_rate_path`, equal to `curve.expected` by test); `G_t` and the risk premium are filters of it. Other bloc outputs, partner exchange rates, the oil gap: realised lags, then AR(1) persistence `world_rho_f` 0.93, `world_rho_p` 0.95, `world_rho_oil` 0.98. | Validated: the sequential algorithm equals the perfect-foresight network to 1e-6 when the projections are exact (W1-09; K = 240 plus tail, D29); with these projections AE5 troughs move by +/-0.1 between persistences 0.90 and 0.97. |
| D18 | US inputs | `y_US = 100 * okun_ratio * (u*_t - mean(u_{t-1..t-3}))` (`okun_ratio` 2.0 as in the ABM's own rule). US policy deviation `ius = 100 (policy_rate - i_inf) - world_ius_offset` pp with `i_inf = current_r_star(w) + pi_lr` (the neutral nominal rate `update_curve` uses); the expected deviation path is `ex[k] = 100 (expected_rate_path[k] - i_inf) - world_ius_offset d_k`, `d_k = 0.97^k` (D28). Without the offset the ABM's mean rate (2.5%) sits 0.4 pp below `i_inf` (2.9%) and the blocs inherit a permanent easing. [assumed] | The ABM has no online HP gap; Okun is already in its Taylor rule; neutral-rate references other than `i_inf` leave a permanent tail in the expected path (tested). |
| D19 | Official Treasury demand | No duration netting (it would double count). Explicit term with total coefficients `treasury_demand.official_demand_term_premium_coefficients` (13 bp stock, 20 bp transitory per 1% of GDP, half-life 12 months). The official tranche is a sub-account of RoW; only world-block flows (reserve-manager rule, trend, `OfficialTreasury` shock) change it, executed by ledger transfers with banks; QE keeps the master mechanism and is not netted. A `rebalance_row` hook keeps the executed flows out of the private RoW book (rev 8: it takes the cash out of the deposits and puts the sold bonds back, D45). The tranche is a memo split that grows with trend nominal GDP (no trade), so the stock term `FO/GDP` has no baseline drift (section 2.7). | QE sales by RoW to the Fed must not raise the term premium (finance 5.3). |
| D20 | Fed purchases from foreign official holders under QE | Test `qe_and_official_terms_add`: 10y response to QE with and without official flows differs by <= 2bp (W4-14). | Evidence that the Fed and official holders are substitutes (finance 5.3). |
| D21 | Global financial factor | `F_t = world_vix_per_unit * f_t` (10 VIX points per unit). World profile sets `fin_factor_rho` 0.83 (`vix_ar1_monthly_level`), `fin_factor_sigma` 0.30, `fin_jump_intensity` 0.06 (rev 7; the rev-6 value 0.035 gives P(VIX rise >= 10 points) = 0.024, below the W4-05 band [0.03, 0.06]; 0.06 gives 0.039, data 0.044), `fin_jump_mean` 1.3, `fin_jump_sd` 0.5, `erp_fin_loading` 0.0028 [derived: -0.63% equity per VIX point at a cap rate of 4.5%]. A user US policy surprise adds `world_vix_per_100bp/10` = 1.3 units of `f_t` per 100bp (injected in `world::after_exog`). Dollar term: `F^{risk}_t = 0.9 F^{risk}_{t-1} + eps^F_t` (asymmetry 0.588 = 1/1.7 on falls). Foreign demand sees `Fex_t = F_t - Fbar - Fpol_t`, the part of `F` not caused by the policy surprise, centred on the unconditional mean `Fbar = world_vix_per_unit fin_factor_mean(cfg)` (rev 7: an uncentred level would be a month-0 reset of the blocs' IS forcing). The injections (policy surprise, `RiskOff`, `BlocDemand{risk_off}`) and the filters `Fpol` and `Frisk` belong to sub-round 4-1 and `world_link_dollar`, not to `world_link_finance`; `F` is the ABM's own `f_t`, a state through burn-in, and only innovations or changes since the base enter levels (1.6b). The injection happens in `after_exog`, after `factors_step` has used `f_t` this month for `tp_level` and `risk_premium`; those two see it from the next month. | finance 4.2, 4.3: 13 points per 100bp; dollar +0.176%/point; asymmetric 1.7x. |
| D22 | Cross-border positions | Memo balance sheet per bloc (9 stocks) tied to the ledger: RoW Treasuries and RoW deposits are split by bloc from the ledger; all other stocks are memo with reinvested earnings and zero net cash flows, so the identity `dNIIP = CA_ledger + memo primary income + valuation` holds exactly (rev 10: the interest and auction flows of the ledger are accumulated by hooks, D52). Fund foreign-equity sleeve (real, ledger-backed) is sub-stage 4e, off by default. The ledger itself carries only the Treasury and deposit stocks and the goods and interest flows with RoW; memo primary income is not paid through it. | Keeps the ledger exact; the sleeve is needed only for the spillback gate W4-18. |
| D23 | OPEC+ composition mismatch (trade report excludes Malaysia/Brunei) | Accepted and documented: GDP, oil and finance use 21 members; the trade matrices use the researcher's OPEC+ without Malaysia/Brunei (RoW). | Trade REPORT section 2; effect on the US is second-order. |
| D24 | Initial tariff state | Zero increment over the 2024 stance (the ABM and b7 treat the tariff as incremental). The October 2026 stance is a preset (Yale partner effective rates, `tariffs.yale_partner_effective_rates_end_2026_pre_substitution_pct`, taken as effective rates). | One consistent convention. |
| D25 | China regime | `uip_w` 0.85 (managed float, between GPM7's 0.34 and free float). Reference simulation, CN GDP trough: -0.71 at 0.70, -0.60 at 0.85, -0.51 at 1.0 (research quarterly network: -0.86 at 0.34, -0.16 at 1.0 with a different parameter set). | Blocs REPORT 2.5 sensitivity; W1-20 band [-0.90, -0.10]. |
| D26 | Import price numeraire | Origin import prices are `PT_us` (domestic tradables price, lagged one month) times `exp(r_j)`, where `r_j` is a pass-through of the real exchange rate; foreign inflation reaches US prices only through exchange rates. | Keeps relative prices stationary; no drift between the ABM's goods and services inflation. |
| D27 | US-to-bloc trade channel | In coupled mode `F_i` uses the US import-demand gap from bloc `i` (activity gap plus the import-intensity deviation from its slow benchmark, 3-month EWMA, clamped +/-10%; S2-R5) instead of `y_US` (section 2.3). Switch `world_us_import_channel`. | Lets tariffs and the dollar reach China's output. |
| D28 | Baseline offset of the US policy deviation | `ius_t = 100 (policy_rate_t - i_inf_t) - world_ius_offset`, with `world_ius_offset` a fixed config constant in pp (not a per-run estimate): the mean of `100 (policy_rate - i_inf)` over months 0-239 and over the 12 seeds of master runs, **measured once** by `scripts/world/measure_ius_offset.py` = **-0.40** (SE 0.09; 32 seeds -0.46, across-seed sd 0.53) on the joint2-S6 runs (20,000 households; S6 is the current default) [verify@stage1: stage-1 step 0 re-measures it on the `M3` binary, command in section 3]. The expected path is shifted by the same offset decaying at the path's own rate, `ex[k] = 100 (expected_rate_path[k] - i_inf) - world_ius_offset d_k` with `d_k = 0.97^k` (`rule_path_decay^k` when `expected_rate_path` uses it), so that `ex[0] = ius_t` and `ex[k] -> 0` (a constant shift of the whole path would leave the blocs expecting a rise to neutral for ever) [assumed]. W1-31 stays a gate; W2-15 adds the `z~` centring gate. | The system is linear, so the shift removes the steady state at no cost. Reference run with real ABM series: without it z is -2.6..-5.3% and y up to +0.63 (W1-31 fails); with it all /z/ <= 0.34, /y/ <= 0.02 on seeds 1-12 and /z/ <= 1.32, /y/ <= 0.14 on seeds 13-32 (not used to measure the offset). |
| D29 | Forcing horizon | K = 240 for every forcing kernel plus a tail term `tail[e] f_e[K-1]`, `tail[e] = sum_{k=240}^{479} IR_e[k]` (constant continuation of each forcing path beyond K), solved with T = 720. K = 240 alone fails the stage-1 test (persistence 0.99 and permanent addend, tolerance 0.05, against K = 480: JP 0.106 and 1.39, OPEC+ 0.21 on a permanent IS addend); with the tail the worst error is 0.022 (JP IS, persistence 0.99; 0.0224 against K = 960). For a permanent addend the tail is by definition the K = 240..479 sum, so against K = 480 that case is an identity: it is tested against K = 960 (0.015, JP IS; K = 480 itself is within 0.015 of K = 960). **S1-d:** profile first; only if total cost is above 5% reduce K for the S equation only, recompute `tail[S]` as the sum from the new K to 479, and re-run the test; K is never reduced for IS, PC or rule (K = 120 for the rule equation misses by 0.90 / 3.69 with the old tail kept and by 0.21 with the tail recomputed; for S the recomputed tail passes narrowly, 0.042). | The UIP sum is undiscounted and the kernels do not decay quickly with k; permanent `Dollar` shocks, held oil gaps and long-half-life `BlocDemand` exist (review problem 5; VERIFY items 4 and 6). |
| D30 | Validation labels | **G** gate (out-of-sample, counted as evidence), **F** fit check (must pass; the parameter was set or searched on that statistic, or the value is by construction), **R** report, **E** engineering identity. In-sample rows are relabelled F (43 rows); reports show F and G in separate tables and summary counts quote G only (26 rows after rev 3, 27 after rev 7: W4-32 is added). W3-05/W3-06 are F although D5 calls them data bands: oil REPORT 7.5 shows that steps 1-3 of the oil calibration fit exactly these bands and moments. W4-07, W4-08 and W4-15 are F: W4-07/W4-08 are the V05/V06 targets with the same bands as the F rows W1-17/W1-19 and the same levers, and the stage-4 selection tunes `world_kus_mult`/`world_lam_rp_mult` and the China risk loading on them. **Tuning rule for G rows:** a G row is never tuned; W2-01 and W3-15 are G rows whose only levers are those of D13 and D5, and a row on which a lever fires is reported F for that run; W3-07 and W3-14 are G and are *not* selection criteria (the oil profile is chosen on the F rows W3-01..W3-04; W3-14 has no lever); W1-31 is scored on the 20 holdout seeds 13-32 (the offset was measured on seeds 1-12). Borderline rows kept G and flagged: W3-09 (the OPEC+ rule parameters come from `opec_episodes`, which includes December 2008) and W4-03 (its expected value is the sourced gamma_JP x 14). | Review problem 22; VERIFY item 10. |
| D31 | `UsCredibility` | `UsCredibility{tp_bp, months, dollar_pct}`: from the shock month, with profile `p_t` = 1 for `months` months and then `0.5^((t - months + 1)/3)`: (i) a term-premium shock `tp_bp * p_t` loaded on the tenors by `TP_SLOPE[j]/TP_SLOPE[3]` (rev 8, D46: the 10y exact, the 3m zero); (ii) `ln(1 - dollar_pct/100) * p_t` added to the common dollar premium `q_t` (section 2.6). `g_{j,t}` is not touched. The blocs see the profile through the exact projected path `dollar_proj` (section 2.6), not an AR(1): at 0.99 a month the bloc feedback on the impact-month `z` is +0.32 instead of +0.029 and the hand value fails. The April 2025 episode is `{tp_bp 22, months 3, dollar_pct 3.3}`. Hand-value test `credibility_dollar_hand_value`. W4-04 keeps its band and is labelled F (the dollar response is an input). | A term-premium shock alone does not enter bloc UIP, and `g` is about 0 in baseline (review problem 17). |
| D32 | Official flows | Monthly-change rule `N_j,t = eta * 100 (ln D_t - ln D_{t-1}) * H_j,t-1/k_FO` (eta -0.0023: the sum over any 6 months equals `eta * x6`), no Japan-specific factor. Hand value for a one-month +10% step: -0.0023 x 9.531 x 3773 = -$82.7bn (test -83 +/- 3). W4-13 (sd of official flows) is report-only: no noise term is added to hit a moment. | The earlier rule applied the 6-month coefficient every month (six-fold overshoot, review problem 19). |
| D33 | b7-transmission in world dollar mode | M-F and M-X bind. `bounded_rate_differential(x/100, fx_rate_knee, fx_rate_sat_scale) * 100` is applied to `ius` and to every `ex[k]` before they enter `fS` and `fR` (identity at the default `fx_rate_sat_scale` 0); the dollar premium `q_t` uses the exact `psi_step` call of `exchange_rate` including M-X's `z` and `fx_mp_prev` (section 2.6). **b7-transmission is part of `M3`:** joint calibration 3 merges it at its step 0 with every key off, so `pin_path_on`, `pinned_expected`, `fx_mp_*` and `bounded_rate_differential` exist at `M3` and stage 1 implements all three branches of `expected_rate_path` and the M-F bound. *Fallback:* if `M3` lacks them, stage 1 implements branches 1 and 2 only (the test count is unchanged), and branch 3 and the M-F bound move to stage 4 step 0, which then starts only after the merge. Stage 4's first step re-reads the merged `fx.rs` and updates the call before coding. W4-28 adds the pin rows. **Rev 7 (checked against `ccdcc48`):** `fx.rs` is unchanged since 6faf872 except that `#[inline(never)]` was removed from `bounded_rate_differential` (`fx.rs:150`); the defaults are M-X on (`fx_mp_premium` 1.25, `fx_mp_cap` 0.03; joint 3), M-P on (`pin_path_on` true, `pin_exit_hazard` 0.0417, `pin_exit_decay` 0.9; joint 4) and **M-F off** (`fx_rate_knee` 0.02, `fx_rate_sat_scale` 0.0; the bound is the identity unless a profile sets the scale, as W4-28 does). | Review problem 20; VERIFY item 5; pre-flight rev 7. |
| D34 | `ImportTariff` semantics | `ImportTariff{pct}` keeps its set-level meaning (`w.shocks.tariff = pct/100`, replaced not added). Bilateral `Tariff{partner, sector, pct, retaliate}` components add on top of it. tau is a decimal everywhere (0.25 = 25%). `tau_eff` is computed per channel: household channel over sectors with `cpi_tradable_weight != 0`, firm channel over sectors with `tradable_weight != 0` (Food is in the second set only). | Review problem 9. |
| D35 | W4-02 risk persistence | One AR(1) risk term with persistence `world_fx_risk_rho` (default 0.9; lever grid {0.90, 0.93, 0.95}). Impact and 12-month rows are fit checks; the **6-month row is report-only**: the data path (+2.9 / +3.6 / +1.0) is hump-shaped and an AR(1) is monotone (1.56 / 1.84 / 2.04 at the 6-month row, record T0+5, for rho 0.90 / 0.93 / 0.95; rev 7: the earlier 1.40 / 1.71 / 1.94 used the exponent 6, see the horizon convention of 3.0). S4-c cannot fire on the 6-month row; no band is lowered. *Deviation from D-R10:* the ruling calls the impact and 12-month rows G; they are F by the D-R4 logic (the dollar loading gamma comes from the same regressions as the targets). | Review problem 18; VERIFY item 13. |
| D36 | Burn-in and the reported run | The blocs, the oil market and their states step in shadow through the 144 burn-in months; **no link acts during burn-in** (`trade_active`, `oil_active`, `dollar_active`, `finance_active` all include `reported(w)`), so burn-in is the legacy ABM. `reported(w) = !(w.tick_offset == 0 && w.tick < w.cfg.burn_in_months)` (the negation of `demography::in_burn_in`; a world with `burn_in_months = 0`, such as `tiny_world`, is reported from tick 0). At the **end of the last burn-in month** (`WorldBlock::settle`, after that month's settlement; a world without burn-in captures at its first `world::step`) the **trade bases** are captured from the ABM's own values (S2-R1, F1, F5): `fx_0`, `p_world,0`, `PT_0`, the sector price ratios `base_s`, the legacy import level `imp_level` (`goods.import_rel` of that month), the legacy exports of that month over the block's base export formula (`kappa_s`), the bloc cross-section at the base (`z_base`), Energy's competitor factor at the base (`world_f0`) and the volume bases. **No ABM state is overwritten**: `w.monetary.fx_base` and `trade_import_z` continue from the burn-in, so the terms ratio carries on from the burn-in (the earlier overwrite and reset are removed). The stage-1 bases (`ln S_j,0`, `ln P_j,0`, `PT_0`) are still captured at the first reported world step (section 1.6). The dynamic terms of section 2.5 read the change since the base, so they start at the base-month levels, and a dollar shock at reported month 0 is measured against the base (the month-1 workaround is removed). With the dollar link on, `w.row.fx = fx_0 exp(ln D_t)` (`D_0 = 1` at the base). | Review problem 12; VERIFY item 8. |
| D37 | Stage-4 continuity (S2-R1 and S3-R1 applied) | No stage-4 state is reset at reported month 0 and none needs a splice (section 1.6b): the dollar is base-relative (`ln D` from `lnS_b`, `fx_0` captured at the end of the last burn-in month), premium states are innovation-only or run in shadow and are read as changes since the base (`psi`, `Frisk`), the official tranche and the memo positions are captured at the base month from the ABM's own ledger and GDP, the sleeve is carved out of existing equity. The rule is: a link adds the change of a block state since the base to an ABM quantity, never its level. | Stage 2 failed on a month-0 reset (S2-R1), stage 3 needed a splice (S3-R1); the pre-flight note (not published) measured the weighted bloc `z` at +14.9 % (sd 11.2) at the base, so a level-based dollar would have jumped by that amount. |
| D38 | Dollar interface (rev 7) | Section 2.6: two phases a month (premium before the bloc step, index after it); `UsInputs` gains `dollar_delta`, `proj_on`, `dollar_proj[K]` (the projected `q + g + cred`: `qpsi` at `fx_psi_rho`, `gbar` at `world_fx_risk_rho`, the credibility path exact); the blocs see the broad addend `a_t = delta_t + q_t + gbar_t` [assumed: the spread of `gamma_j` acts on `lnS_j` and trade only]; `Fex = F - Fbar - Fpol` with a `theta_f` term added to `fIS`; `Frisk` runs in shadow and is read as a change since the base. | `fx.rs` is unchanged in substance since 6faf872, but the merged `UsInputs` and `step_bloc` carry one addend with one decay and no `fex` term; an AR(1) projection of the credibility term fails its own hand value (+0.32 against +0.029 of bloc feedback). |
| D39 | Units of the official block (rev 7) | All official-block quantities are real-world-equivalent: model amounts / `k_FO`, GDP as `GDP^rw = world_gdp_rw_bn trend_gdp_month(t)/trend_gdp_month(b)`; the tranche is `nu x official_share_j x tic_share_j x RoW_MV_b` with `nu = 3773.1 / 3786.28` so that the official share of RoW holdings is the sourced 40.80 %; `k_FO = FO_b / 3773.1` is seed-specific; trades are executed in model units. | The ABM's RoW holds 42.8 % of GDP at the base against 28.4 % in the data (range 34-66 %): conversions by GDP would scale every term-premium effect by 1.2-2.3 between seeds and break the hand values (-10 bp, -4 bp, -83 bn). |
| D40 | Positions at the base (rev 7) | Memo stocks are scaled by `k_GDP,b` of the base month (rev 10: the trend GDP of the base month, D52; GDP at creation is about 68 % of it). The nine stocks cover 80 % of the IIP assets and 82 % of its liabilities: memo NIIP = -19.46 tn = **-59.8 %** of 32.56 tn, not -68.9 % (reserves, derivatives and non-bank other investment are not modelled). W4-21 is restated (memo coverage identity E; model `niip_pct_gdp` reported); W4-22 expected -8.66 % (`ln 1.10`), not -9.2. | the pre-flight note (not published) section 5: the nine research stocks sum to a different NIIP from the aggregate the row quoted. |
| D41 | Stage-4 protocol (rev 7, pre-registered) | Section 3.0: horizon convention `T0 + h - 1`; matched cells (W, E4x, L4x) with identical ABM keys; seeds per row family; the confirmatory rule on fresh seeds 1001-1064; harness pins (`warmup_months` 0 in every request, `ECONSIM_WARMUP_MONTHS=0`, the response must lack `warmup_months`); G4 is evaluated once per sub-round on its final switch combination. | Stage-3 lessons S3-R2 (horizon), S3-R3 (power), S2-R2 (pairing); the server default warm-up is 24 since joint calibration 4. |
| D42 | Sub-rounds | Stage 4 is four sub-rounds, each with its switch, gate subset, stop rules and independent review: 4-1 dollar and risk factor (`world_link_dollar`), 4-2 official demand and term premium (`world_link_finance`), 4-3 retaliation and positions (`world_retaliation`, `world_positions`), 4-4 Fund sleeve (`world_fund_foreign_equity`, optional). 4-1 is about the size of stage 2 (one new module of about 700 lines, five touched files, 25 tests). | One implementer round for the whole stage would be four times the size of stage 2 and mix the riskiest identity exposures (`exchange_rate` skip, `update_curve` hook, ledger flows). |
| D43 | Sub-round 4-2 with the dollar link off (rev 8, S4-R15) | 4-2 is built and measured from the list-A head with `world_link_dollar` false and `world_link_trade` true. `ln D` is the ABM's own dollar (`ln(w.row.fx/fx_0)` of the trade base); the injections of 4-1 do not run, so the VIX term sees the ABM's own `f_t` only (`RiskOff`, `BlocDemand{risk_off}` and the policy-surprise injection are 4-1 functions of the dollar link); the dollar-mode rows are deferred to the combined run after list B: W4-12 in dollar mode (-60.3), W4-13 and W4-10 in dollar mode, the cumulative W4-32 prefix, the official purchases that follow a `RiskOff`. The code reads `w.row.fx` and `w.exog.fin_factor` only, so it is valid in both modes. | The 4-1 link failed W4-28 and W4-32 on spec design gaps; the official block does not depend on it (S4-R15); the reviewer's condition (a) asked for a restatement of every dollar-mode row. |
| D44 | The tranche on the realised trend; per-bloc cap (rev 8) | `trend_gdp_month` is the ABM's `tau <- tau (1 + g/12) + 0.03 (GDP - tau)`, read in `world::step` as `plan_spending` of the previous month left it; the tranche is kept as the memo path `h_j,b tau_t/tau_b` plus a deviation `dH_j` that is 0.0 bit for bit until a flow executes, so `tp_FO` is exactly 0.0 in the baseline and the finance link with every driver at zero equals the link off (I5). A bloc cannot sell more than it holds (`N_j >= -H_j,t-1/k_FO`): the CN trend (-80 a year) exhausts the CN tranche (585 $bn rw) at month 102 (93-111), after which the baseline term premium settles near +19 bp instead of rising to +49 bp (the rev-7 figure was an uncapped extrapolation with negative holdings). The VIX term is `v_vix mean_6(F - Fbar)` of the level including the current month [assumed: the research says $1.05bn a month per point, zero beyond six months]. | Probes on 64 E4a runs and 12-seed prototype cells (PREFLIGHT 2-3). |
| D45 | Execution counterparties and the RoW book (rev 8) | Official purchases are bought from the banks and then the Fund (`trade_with` with the Fund last: the banks alone hold none of the bonds in some worlds and `trade_with` splits its ask equally, so a bank-only list executed 5-45 % of a +100 purchase in 4 of 12 seeds); official sales go to the banks (`trade_with_banks`, always accepted). `extra` = cumulative net official sales at market value; `rebalance_row` works on `dep - extra` and `bonds_value + extra` (D19): the rev-7 hook shifted the target only, and the `bonds_value/12` term of the target then undid `1/13` of every sale (6.5 % measured at month 12). | Prototype cells with both hooks (PREFLIGHT 2.4). |
| D46 | `UsCredibility` term-premium part (rev 8) | Registered in `world::after_exog` (before the curve, so the shock month's curve carries it) while `world_link_finance`; `tp_bp/1e4 p(age) TP_SLOPE[j]/TP_SLOPE[3]` on tenor `j` (the 10y exact: +22.0 bp at ages 0-2 for the April 2025 episode, the 2y +4.4, the 5y +11.0, the 30y +28.6, the 3m zero) [assumed: D31 says "all tenors"; a flat 22 bp would also move the 3m bill, the 2y rate and the business-loan base `0.5 y2 + 0.5 y5` (`banking.rs:139-142`): +22 bp there against +7.7]. The dollar term needs `world_link_dollar`, the term-premium part `world_link_finance`; each works without the other. | The convention of every other term-premium term in `update_curve` (`TP_SLOPE`). |
| D47 | Baseline neutrality of the bloc base state (rev 9, list B) | The dollar link reads each bloc's real exchange rate against its **conditional forecast from the base month**, `z_fc,j(m)` (m = 0 the first reported month), not against its base value: `lnS_j = (z_j - z_fc,j)/100 + (lnP_j - lnP_j,b) - (ln PT_{t-1} - ln PT_b) + a_j + zeta_j` (2.6) and the trade pass-through reads `zrel_j = z_j - z_fc,j + 100 a_j` (2.5). `z_fc` is the path the twelve coupled blocs follow from their captured state under the US inputs they themselves expect at the base (2.6, "The conditional forecast"): the base month's expected policy path moved forward month by month, the activity, oil, quantity and non-policy-risk terms decaying at the persistences the blocs project them with, no innovation, no shock, no dollar addend. It is computed once at the base capture (`WorldBlock::forecast`, 480 months, then held), is a function of the base state only and is shared by the two arms of a pair (an `Arc`). `z - z_fc` is an innovation-only state in the sense of 1.6b: the block's own forecast error, zero at the base and mean zero in the baseline. Switch `world_dollar_neutral` (default true; false is the rev-7 form, kept for identity I6). **Why not the rev-7 form or the review's twin.** The rev-7 form read `z - z_b`: the block's deterministic unwinding from a base-month misalignment (weighted `z` +7.6, sd 11) moved the ABM's real dollar 4.8 / 6.5 / 8.7 points below its own `c_t` and added +0.31 to core PCE (W4-32). The review's twin (`fix_prototype_TW_unforced_z*.diff`, `FIX2`) read `z` against the path of **zero** US inputs: that removes the unwinding but also treats the base-month *level* of the inputs (an expected policy gap, the state of `G`) as news arriving at month 0, a step of the weighted `z - z_ref` of +1.5 points (sd 2.4, max 7.9 over seeds 1-64) that W4-29 counts (mean 2.19, 8 seeds above 4.0, trade clause 3x the rev-7 size); the forecast step is -0.05 (sd 0.34, max 1.1). The rule of 1.6b is kept: a link adds a change, never a level. | PREFLIGHT 2-3; `fix_prototype_*.diff`; section 7 item 17. |
| D48 | Bound on the total US-rate excess return in the bloc dollar equation (rev 9) | In `fS` the expected excess return of the bloc currency against the dollar is `T_k = ex[k] + lambda_rp,i gp[k]` (pp a year; 2.2.2). Rev 9 bounds the total: `fS[k] = -( M(T_k) + u_t rho_rp^k )`, `M(x) = x` for `x <= kappa_f`, `kappa_f + L_f tanh((x - kappa_f)/L_f)` above (the b7-transmission M-F function, `fx::bounded_rate_differential`), `kappa_f` = `world_rp_sat_knee` 0.02 (2.0 pp), `L_f` = `world_rp_sat_scale` 0.015 (1.5 pp), ceiling 3.5 pp. Active while `world_link_dollar` is on (burn-in included), the identity otherwise; `G`, the IS term `theta gp` and `fR` (which keeps `exb`) are unchanged. **Source of the number, fixed before any list-B run.** (i) The knee and scale are the b7-transmission M-F constants (D33; `fx_rate_knee`, `fx_rate_sat_scale`; CALIBRATION "M-F bounded UIP"), fixed before stage 4 and used unchanged by the W4-28 profile, so list B adds no free number. (ii) The bound is put on the total because the equation prices the whole US-rate-driven excess return and D33 bounded only its first part, so `lambda_rp gp` re-opened an unbounded response (W4-28 30.5 / 54.3 in 4-1). (iii) The knee is the peak total of the calibration shock: `lambda_rp` was fitted on a +100 bp, four-quarter US shock (blocs REPORT 4.4), for which `T = 1.0 + lambda_rp 0.590` is 1.89 (AE, lambda 1.5) to 2.18 pp (EME, 2.0), `G` after 12 months of a unit gap being `1 - 0.8^(12/3)` = 0.590; the linear loading is used over the range it was identified on and saturates beyond it. (iv) At the ceiling the bloc equation prices the same maximum as the ABM's own M-F-bounded rule (3.5 pp), so the link cannot respond to a US rate path more than the calibrated ABM dollar does. The reviewer's clamp values (1 pp and 2 pp on `gp`) were found on the pin rows and are not used. Off with `world_rp_sat_scale` 0. | Review 3.1 (prototypes P1-P4); blocs REPORT 4.4; D33; PREFLIGHT 4. |
| D49 | List B protocol, stop rules and two readings fixed before any run (rev 9) | Section 3.0 "Sub-round 4-1b protocol": cells `E4d`, `L4d`, `W`; fresh seeds (default runs 3201-3264, confirmatory 3301-3364; events 1201-1216 with W4-26 1201-1232, event confirmatory to 1248; pins 2201-2216, confirmatory pins 2217-2232); stop rules S4-n to S4-q: a failure of W4-28 or W4-32 (the confirmatory run included) leaves the dollar link off for good, there is no further list, stage 5 records it. Two readings, **[assumed; ruling S4-R26 needed]**: W4-29 clause 2 excuses a seed whose matched cell is itself above 3.0 % and where the link exceeds it by at most 0.5 (the premium innovation is common to both arms); G4 scores the per-seed statistics (`u_ac1`, `tot_lt25`) with an effect floor of 0.2 of their cross-seed sd in the matched cell besides Z (S2-R4 and S3-R7 asked for such a floor to be decided before a run, not after). | PREFLIGHT 7; stage-4-1 review 3.2, 3.6. |
| D51 | Retaliation rule, restated (rev 10) | D9 with these readings. (i) The lag is a delay: the target reads the average US duty `lag_j` months ago [assumed]; the research writes `max_{k=0..lag_j}`, which passes a rise at once whatever the lag and so makes every lag invisible to a permanent duty. (ii) `taubar_{US->j}` is the average of the active flagged duty components over the nine sectors, weighted by the fixed 2024 import values `imports_base[j][s]`: a function of the components only (no ABM state, no feedback). (iii) `mu_up` 1 and `mu_down` 0.1 are constants; `world_ret_eps_sr`, `world_ret_eps_lr`, `world_ret_speed` are the keys; blocs without a lag entry get 1 month [assumed] (only RoW has `kappa` > 0). (iv) With the switch on and no flagged `Tariff` every retaliation state is 0.0 and `R` is 1.0, so the output is bit-identical to the switch off. (v) The metric `ret_tau[j]` is the mean of `tau*_{j,s}` over the goods sectors Energy..Tech weighted by `x0_{s,j}`, `exports_goods_bn[j]` the goods exports to the bloc. | The research formula as written has no delay for a permanent duty; the fixed weights and the neutrality make the rule a function of the shocks alone (continuity, D37). |
| D52 | Positions, restated (rev 10) | `k_GDP,b = 12 tau_b / world_gdp_rw_bn` with the trend GDP of the base month (the `tau_b` of the official block, D44); the base month's actual GDP differs from the trend by 1.5 % (sd across seeds, measured) and would put that noise into every memo stock. The valuation uses the change of the ABM's broad dollar for every bloc when `world_link_dollar` is false (the block has no bilateral nominal rates then; the earlier text "the block's own bilateral rates" held only with the link on) and the dollar module's `ln S_j` when it is true. The ledger-backed Treasury stock is valued at constant face with a matured bond at par, plus the auction term (RoW pays par for a new issue, whose price is not 1 when a tenor's yield is below zero and the coupon is floored at 0); the coupon, the deposit interest and the auction purchases are accumulated by hooks H19-H21 because the end-of-month states cannot give them. The noise `eta` is drawn on `WORLD_FX` at index `16 + j`. | An exact identity needs the ledger's own flows: without the auction term the residual is 2e-5 of monthly GDP in months with a negative 3m yield, with it 4e-14. |
| D53 | W4-24 statistics on goods (rev 10) | The retaliation rows are scored on goods exports (Energy..Tech) per `ln(1 + ret_tau)`: the services sectors have `w_s` 0, and the model's exports to China are 82 % goods against 74.8 % in the 2024 table, so the all-sector figure depends on the mix (-21.4 % at the 2024 mix and -23.9 % measured, against -28.6 % and -29.0 % for goods). The W4-24b export level is the goods figure. | A definition correction made before any 4-3 run (the S4-R12 form); it needs the metric `exports_goods_bn`. |
| D54 | 4-3 changes no default run (rev 10) | Neither switch changes an ABM quantity in a run without a flagged `Tariff`: the positions are memo and the retaliation factor is exactly 1.0. The 4-3 prefix of W4-32 is therefore an identity (I7), not a statistical gate, and the finance link stays out of the 4-3 cells (S4-R24). | A G row whose difference is exactly zero tests nothing; the identity is the stronger check. |
| D55 | Stage-5 protocol (rev 11, pre-registered) | Section 3.0 "Stage-5 protocol": a cumulative ladder of profiles **T** (`b8-world-s5a.json`: trade, retaliation and positions on; dollar, finance and oil off), **D** (`b8-world-s5b.json`: T plus the dollar link with the M-F keys, S4-R30) and **F** (`b8-world-s5c.json`: D plus the finance link); cells `L0` (the J4 default, no keys), `W'` (block off with the profiles' ABM keys), T, D, F. Each rung is judged against the rung below as its matched cell (S2-R2: T against W', D against T, F against D); the rows that decide the default (W5-01, W5-02) compare a rung with `L0`. Outcomes: **ship** (rung rows pass), **profile** (rows pass, or a G row misses by less than 2 SE of the pooled estimate after the confirmatory rule - the near-miss rule - and the link ships only as a named profile) and **off** (an E row that fails, or a G row that misses by 2 SE or more). **The pre-registered default set is {block, trade, retaliation, positions}**; the dollar link (with its M-F keys) and the finance link ship as profiles whatever their rows say, and are not candidates for the default set in this round (question Q3 of the pre-flight). The oil link is measured only through the early gates of D59. Fresh seeds: default runs **6101-6164** (confirmatory 6165-6228), events **1401-1464** (first 1401-1416, extension 1417-1448, confirmatory 1449-1464), pins **2301-2332** (first 2301-2316), oil **10001-10800** and **20001-24000**. Stop rules S5-a..S5-o (Stage 5). No lever is used in stage 5 except the pre-authorised S4-h lever of the finance rung. | The single sentence of the old default rule ties one flip to every row at once; the sub-round design of stage 4 decided per link, and the matched cells make that decision independent of the realism program's ABM keys. The seeds 6001-6032 (4-2 finance probes) and 1301-1364 (inside the S3-R3 W3-14 set) named in the dispatch were not fresh; see the pre-flight note (not published) section 2. |
| D56 | Rows re-specified before any stage-5 run (rev 11) | Each takes effect only by orchestrator ruling before the first registered run; no band moves, and the superseded statistic stays on record beside the new one. (i) **W1-31** is scored on the registered default runs of cell T (64 seeds) with the D57 constants, not on seeds 13-32. (ii) **W2-06** (the b7 tariff set T1-T9) runs at 48 event seeds in every cell with the trade link. (iii) **W2-10** is the through-origin slope of the paired import response on the paired GDP response (elasticity convention, band [1.5, 2.3], reported). (iv) **W3-14** v2: the ratio of the link-on cross-seed mean to a cached legacy benchmark, point rule +-3 %. (v) **W4-07 / W4-08: REJECTED by orchestrator ruling S5-R5** (the `pin7` re-specification was chosen after both pins had been seen, which D41 forbids). The rows stay on `pol100l` (PolicyShift 100bp, 24m; month 24 per pp of the mean policy gap over months 1-12), F class, recorded failures under S4-R28 (list B 1.751 / 1.759); the `pin7` and `pin10` values are reported as W4-39. The EME set CN, MX, IN, KR, BR is fixed as stated. (vi) **W4-26** P1-P5 are gated on the seed-mean IRF (the convention of `irf_check.py`, the source of the bands); the per-seed values are reported. (vii) **W5-04**: a preset whose needed link does not ship is withdrawn, not failed (new column "Needs"); the "exports to China" row is the goods statistic of D53 with the elasticity band of its literature column (section 5). (viii) The G2 spike guard keeps its rule and prints the exact binomial probability beside it. (ix) The rows of the ECB, BoJ and PBoC presets that name a US effect near zero (US GDP trough +-0.10 / +-0.05; PBoC: US exports at m12 > 0) are reported, not gated: the jackknife SE of a 36-month trough of the paired mean path is 0.08-0.10 at 16 seeds and about 0.06 at 48, so no seed count the protocol allows decides them. | Each is a defect of the statistic found in a record or in a power calculation, not a result of stage 5: the pre-flight section 6 gives the evidence line by line (a decayed response read per pp of a persistent shock; a single matched arm carrying 1.4 points of the SE; a level band that does not follow from its literature column; a 16-seed power on a coin-flip row; a +-0.05 band under an SE of 0.08). |
| D57 | Centring constants of the coupled import-demand term (rev 11; S2-R7) | `world_import_gap_offset`, twelve per-bloc constants in pp (bloc order), subtracted from the **clamped** term in `TradeState::pre_bloc` once it is active (reported month 13 on): `term = clamp(100 ewma, -10, 10) - offset_j`. Empty (the default) is the identity. Measured on exploratory seeds 1-64, 20,000 x 240, cell `b8-world-s4a.json`, months 13-239 of `world.us_import_gap`: **1.983, 1.858, 1.802, 2.933, 1.163, 1.885, 3.007, 2.102, 2.125, 1.822, 1.814, 2.577** (SE 0.28-0.46 each). Validated: length 0 or 12, each finite and in [-10, 10]. They ride in the profiles `s5a`, `s5b` and `s5c`, not in `Config::default`. The stage-5 first step re-measures them on seeds 1-12 and keeps them unless a value is more than 2 SE away (the S4-R10 trigger). | S2-R7 asked for a measured constant and a W1-31 re-run before the trade link can ship. A prototype with constants measured on seeds 33-64 centres the holdout 13-32 to 0.28 / 1.74 (bands 0.5 / 3.0; without them 0.62 / 3.74) and moves the US baseline by less than 0.05 pp in every rate (exports -0.28 %). The term sits on its +-10 clamp in 7-40 % of months, so only its mean can be centred. |
| D58 | Profile family and the legacy profile (rev 11) | `b8-world-s5a.json` = `b8-world-s4c.json` plus the D57 constants; `b8-world-s5b.json` = `b8-world-s4e.json` plus `fx_rate_knee` 0.02 and `fx_rate_sat_scale` 0.015 plus the constants; `b8-world-s5c.json` = `b8-world-s5b.json` plus `world_link_finance` true. Fallbacks written now: `b8-world-s5a-nc.json` (S5-b: the coupled channel off), `b8-world-s5c-nd.json` (the finance link on the trade rung, if the dollar rung is off), and the reported lean arm `b8-world-s5a-lean.json` (`b8-world-s5a.json` without the six financial-factor keys) with its block-off reference `b8-world-wl.json` (`{"world_block_enabled": false, "import_price_elasticity": 0.4, "import_elasticity_lr": 1.0}`). `scripts/profiles/legacy_b8off.json` = `{"world_block_enabled": false, "import_price_elasticity": 0.2, "import_elasticity_lr": 0.6, "fin_factor_rho": 0.98, "fin_factor_sigma": 0.0, "fin_jump_intensity": 0.0, "fin_jump_mean": 0.0, "fin_jump_sd": 0.0, "erp_fin_loading": 0.0}`: it resets everything a world profile moves in the ABM with the block off (the spec's rev-3 form named only the two elasticities); identity I9 below. | The financial-factor keys are zero in the J4 default and switched on by every world profile, and the profiles carry import elasticities 0.4 / 1.0 (J4: 0.2 / 0.6); both are ABM keys that act with the block off (cell W' against `L0` on 32 exploratory seeds: policy -0.13 pp, 10-year -0.07 pp, bank capital -0.41 points, which belong to the elasticities (`Wl` against `L0`: bank capital -0.51), and equity price -2.4 %, which belongs to the financial-factor keys (`W'` against `Wl`: -2.6 %)). |
| D59 | The oil link in stage 5 (rev 11) | Not part of the ladder. Early gates, in this order and only on the first event set: W3-11 (48 seeds 1401-1448, shock at month 12; CPI m12, O1-O3), W3-10 (feedback off, 12 seeds), W3-08 ABM stochastic row (48 seeds). **If any gating row of these fails the oil link ships off (S5-o) and nothing else of oil runs.** If they pass, W3-14 v2 (on-arm 10001-10800 at 1,000 households; legacy benchmark by command on 20001-24000 at 50 households), W3-15 and the default-run cells (G1-G4 with the S4-R26 effect floor 0.2 sd for every G4 statistic; G2 at the pooled 128 seeds) follow. The exact-path design record (copy A frozen while an `OilPrice` exact-path shock is active) is sub-round 5-O, optional, outside stage 5. The Jensen term is accepted and reported (cross-seed sd of ln price 0.378 against 0.425, mean ln shift +0.0003). | W3-11 CPI m12 (0.909, 1.6 SE below its band at 48 seeds), W3-08 stochastic (-1.18, 2.2 SE below) and the G2/G4 records of S3-R12 are all recorded fails of G rows; nothing in stage 5 changes the code they depend on, so the cheap rows decide first. |
| D60 | Start states and the site (rev 11; amended rev 13: `us_now` takes the world block with the trade link only, D65; the dollar, finance and oil links stay refused with 400) | `start_state: us_now` and `world_block_enabled: true` exclude each other (the web line's `validate_config` answers 400; steering is untested with the block, the Today's-start rulings note (not published) 4). Stage 5 keeps the exclusion: world features on the site are for the typical start; the site hides them under "Today's US economy". Sub-round **5-U** (optional, after the decision) makes them compatible. A `Tariff` shock posted with the block or the trade link off answers 400 on `/api/compare` (it was a silent no-op). | A silent no-op in a public what-if tool reads as "tariffs do nothing". |
| D61 | The joint default switch (rev 11) | Stage 5 does not change `Config::default`: identity I1 (`9f90c88a...`) holds after stage 5 and the realism program's identity ("keys off = 9f90c88a", RZ-R1) stays true. The default of the world keys and the realism keys is flipped once, together, after a pre-registered joint run: the 2 x 2 factorial {J4 default, world profile, realism profile, both} on realism's reserved seeds 5129-5164 (Tier A, scorecard) and world events/pins not consumed in stage 5; its rows are Tier A in every cell, scorecard severity change <= +2 for "both" against `L0`, the world W5-04 rows and the realism D1/D4/D5 rows on "both", the interaction table and the cost row. The flip commit adds `legacy_defaults` (= `legacy_b8off.json` plus `legacy_realism`) and identity **I9**. | RZ-R1 reserves one default switch "co-ordinated with the world block's stage 5"; the two programs touch the same functions and the world ladder uses most of the G1 core-PCE headroom (J4 1.79 -> 1.89 with trade and 2.04 with the dollar link, of a 2.3 band, on 32 exploratory seeds). |
| D62 | Drift of the block's import price against PT (rev 12; sub-round 5-T2) | The block's origin import prices are `imp_level (PT_{t-1}/PT_0) exp(rbar)` (D26), a price that is stationary against PT. The legacy ABM's import price (`goods::import_pass_through`: the world price, +0.5 % a year, over the dollar, passed through 0.45 in the long run) falls against PT at about 0.38 % a year (64 exploratory seeds, months 12-239: -0.378 %/yr, SE 0.055; the ABM's tradables price rises 1.22 %/yr), and with the D13 import elasticities (0.4 / 1.0) the legacy household import share rises 0.155 pp a year and imports / GDP 0.04 pp a year. The link-on cell is flat (-0.059 %/yr; imports / GDP +0.006 pp a year), so its import price is 3.4 % higher against PT on average over months 12-239 and its imports / GDP 0.37 pp lower (64 seeds; stage 5, 128 registered seeds: -0.433, SE 0.032): this is the whole of the W2-15 dM/GDP miss (REVIEW section 4). **New key `world_import_rel_drift` g** (decimal a year, default 0 = identity, validated in [-0.05, 0.05]): `imp_rel_s` is multiplied by `exp(-g n / 12)`, `n` = reported months since the base (`TradeState::since_base`: 0 in the first reported month, so the continuity of W2-17 is untouched). The profiles `t2a`, `t2b`, `t2c` carry **g = 0.0032**, the mean over 64 exploratory seeds of the per-seed OLS slope (months 12-239) of ln(import_price_index / PT) in cell T minus the same slope in cell W' (+0.320 %/yr, SE 0.034; seeds 1-12: +0.277, SE 0.058); measured once, fixed before any registered run, not a lever. The shocks are unaffected (the factor is the same in both arms of a compare). | Continuity (D37): a link adds the change of a block state since the base to an ABM quantity, never its level or its trend; D26 removed the legacy drift on purpose ("no drift between the ABM's goods and services inflation") and with it a baseline property the rest of J4 was calibrated with. Pre-test (exploratory seeds 1-64, cell `t2a` as built in the 5-T2 pre-flight note (not published)): dM/GDP -0.041 (SE 0.048) against -0.365 (0.041) without it; core PCE 1.825 against W' 1.790 (T 1.935); policy 2.180 against 2.128 (T 2.322); W2-17 unchanged. A level constant (the D57 form) was rejected: it needs a 3.4 % step at the base: on the import share (+3.2 %, 1.5 pp of the household share) it adds about 0.5 pp of GDP to imports in months 0-2, above the W2-17 limit of 0.2; on the import price it adds 0.2 pp at the short-run elasticity 0.4 and a 0.3 % step to the CPI; and either leaves a +-0.4 pp error in the path. The data have no drift against core goods prices (BLS ex-fuel import prices over core goods CPI, 2005-2026: +0.02 %/yr), so the legacy drift is an ABM artefact; restoring it is a continuity choice, and removing it is a realism item (section 7 item 44). |
| D63 | Uniform duty fold (rev 12) | A `Tariff` component with no partner and no sector is added to the set-level duty before the duties by origin and sector are formed (`TradeState::post_bloc_ref`: `t_uni`, then `t_level + t_uni`; the bilateral sum skips such components), so `Tariff{null, null, p, retaliate}` and `ImportTariff{p}` give the same duty in the same floating-point value through every channel (a flagged form also retaliates when `world_retaliation` is on; the duty is the same). Without it the two differ by 1e-16 in `tau_eff` in 16 of 16 seeds at p = 25 (`tau_eff` (household or firm channel) 0.25 against 0.24999999999999994), the runs stay equal to 8e-16 in GDP in 15 of 16 seeds over 36 months, and one seed (1404) leaves the common path at month 18 through a discrete event (0.06 pp of unemployment, 5.81 against 5.87) and ends a point apart: the 0.91 of the floor of W5-06 at 25 % (stage 5). With no uniform component `t_uni` is 0.0 and `t_level + 0.0` is `t_level`: every run without a uniform `Tariff` is byte-identical to stage 5. Pre-test: 16 of 16 seeds bit-identical in every key of both arms (seeds 1401-1416 at 25 %, the fold build), and the `ImportTariff` run of the fold build equals the stage-5 build's in 16 of 16. | W5-06 asked whether the site may use either path; the answer is that it may, and the fold makes the answer a bit-identity (E row) instead of a sampling statement about one seed. Runs that mix a uniform and a bilateral component (`tarwar`) sum the two in a different order and change at the last digit; every such event cell is re-run in 5-T2. |
| D64 | Sub-round 5-T2 (rev 12) | The trade repair: D62, D63 and the re-measured D57 constants (below) on the stage-5 code, one run of rung T on fresh seeds, then rungs D and F as in stage 5 if T ships. Cells `L0`, `W'` (as in D55), `T2`, `D2`, `F2` = the profiles `b8-world-t2a.json`, `-t2b.json`, `-t2c.json` (the keys of `s5a`, `s5b`, `s5c` plus `world_import_rel_drift` 0.0032 and the constants **2.381, 2.485, 2.419, 3.338, 1.838, 2.748, 3.678, 2.837, 2.568, 2.169, 2.264, 3.056**, = the D57 values plus the mean of the centred term in the drift cell on seeds 1-64). Seeds (none used before, the 5-T2 pre-flight note (not published) section 8): default runs **4401-4464**, confirmatory **4465-4528**; events **4601-4616**, extension **4617-4648**, confirmatory **4649-4664**; pins **4701-4716**, confirmatory **4717-4732**. Stop rules S5T-a..S5T-e (sub-round text). **No lever and no second repair round**: g and the constants are fixed here. The band of W2-15 is not changed by this revision (REVIEW S5-R22 records its provenance and the orchestrator's ruling). | The stage-5 trade rung failed one row with every other row passing; the diagnosis is one mechanism (D62), the repair is one number measured on exploratory seeds, and a repair that is allowed to be re-tuned after its registered run is a lever. |
| D65 | Today's start on the dated trade reference (rev 13; numbered D65 because D64 is 5-T2) | The trade block of `us_now` starts from today's US duty by origin and goods group, the import origin shares, the export destination shares and the partners' duty on US goods, at the vintage `2026-08-24` (the Today's-start design note (not published; its content is summarised in CALIBRATION, `world-usnow`)). Installed once at the end of the window (`TradeState::rebase_today`, with the import-demand benchmark moved so the term is continuous). Every later response is measured against it; with no change the block holds today's state and a `Tariff` adds points. Without a reference every expression is the merged one (the default fingerprint and the stage-5 profiles unchanged). Key `start_targets.trade_ref` (`"none"` or a vintage id). The UK retaliates like the euro area (`kappa` 0.2, lag 2; owner choice, section 2.5). | Today's tariffs and trade shares answer the tariff questions on the start the site already shows; the reference is dated and sourced, and the design is measured against it rather than re-fitted. |

### 0.3 Staging in one table

| Stage | Branch | Content | Link switch (default) | Effect on the ABM |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `b8-world-s1` | Bloc models, offline RE solver, network, shadow coupling (US -> blocs only), `BlocPolicy`/`BlocDemand`/`GlobalDemand` shocks, metrics `world` | `world_block_enabled` (false) | none (bit-identical in shadow mode) |
| 2 | `b8-world-s2` | Bilateral trade by sector, origin import prices (dominant currency), exports on foreign income, partner-sector tariffs | `world_link_trade` (false) | imports, exports, CPI import component |
| 3 | `b8-world-s3` | Oil market, exact-path presets, OPEC+/shale/inventories, oil shocks | `world_link_oil` (false) | `exog.oil_price` |
| 4 | `b8-world-s4` (sub-rounds 4-1..4-4, D42) | Bilateral dollar and risk factor, official Treasury demand and term premium, retaliation, memo positions, optional Fund sleeve | `world_link_dollar`, `world_link_finance`, `world_retaliation`, `world_positions`, `world_fund_foreign_equity` (all false) | dollar, term premium, RoW portfolio, exports |
| 5 | `b8-world-s5` | Validation of each link on a cumulative ladder (D55), profiles `b8-world-s5a/b/c`, the centring constants of D57, presets and site cards, the web brief, docs; **`Config::default` does not change** (the default flip is the joint switch of D61) | none of the switches changes its default | none by default (profiles) |
| 5-T2 | `b8-world-s5t2` (from `212af62`) | The trade repair of rev 12: the import-price drift (D62), the uniform duty fold (D63), the re-measured D57 constants, profiles `b8-world-t2a/b/c`; rung T, then D and F, on fresh seeds (D64) | none of the switches changes its default | none by default (profiles) |

---

## 1. Architecture

### 1.1 Files

New submodule `crates/econsim-core/src/world/` (declare `pub mod world;` in `lib.rs`). No new crates (the workspace has only serde, serde_json, rand, rayon).

| File | Stage | Content |
|---|---|---|
| `world/mod.rs` | 1 | `WorldBlock`, `WorldOut`, `Bloc` enum (12), `init_if_enabled`, `after_exog`, `step`, `settle`, switch helpers (`oil_active`, `trade_active`, `dollar_active`, `finance_active`), debug identity check |
| `world/params.rs` | 1 | `WorldParams` (read-only tables, shared through `Arc`), loader from the two embedded JSON files through a `OnceLock<Arc<WorldParams>>` |
| `world/bloc.rs` | 1 | `BlocState`, forcing-path builders (K = 240 plus `tail[e] f_e[K-1]`), `step_bloc`, shock draws, pin tables, price levels, nominal rates, Okun, headline add-on |
| `world/net.rs` | 1 | `UsInputs`, cross-bloc terms (`F_i`, `P_i`), aggregates (PPP and market weights) |
| `world/wmetrics.rs` | 1 | `WorldMetrics` (serde) |
| `world/trade.rs` | 2 | `TradeState`: origin import prices (dominant currency), origin shares, effective tariff, exports on foreign income, retaliation (the rule is added in 4-3: `RetState`, section 2.5) |
| `world/oil.rs` | 3 | `OilParams`, `OilState`, `oil_step`, shock draws, exact-path shadow copy |
| `world/dollar.rs` | 4 | `DollarState`: bilateral rates, common premium, risk term, disconnect noise, broad index |
| `world/finance.rs` | 4 | `FinState`: `F_t`, policy-surprise injection, official tranche, reserve-manager flows, term-premium term, `rebalance_row` extra deposits, Fund sleeve (4e) |
| `world/positions.rs` | 4 | memo positions, income, valuation, CA and NIIP identities |
| `world/data/world_params.json`, `world/data/bloc_solution.json` | 1 | generated, embedded with `include_str!` (`world_params.json` holds both OPEC+ composition sets; `bloc_solution.json` holds C, IR (K = 240) and tail for each bloc) |
| `world/data/golden/*.json`, `*.jsonl` | 1-4 | golden vectors for tests (Python reference output), names in the placement table below |

Python tooling (stage 1 onward): `scripts/world/` holds the 18 reference tools (`paths.py`, `world_ref.py`, `bloc_ir.py`, `bloc_model.py`, `bloc_params.py`, `lin_pf.py`, `re_statespace.py`, `world_net.py`, `us_shock_in2019.py`, `us_shock_spillover.py`, `pin_tables.py`, `pf_monthly_net.py`, `exp_forcing.py`, `exp_forcing_check.py`, `trade_hand.py`, `oil_activity.py`, `measure_ius_offset.py`, `make_layout.py`) plus the four new tools `solve_blocs.py`, `build_world_params.py`, `make_golden.py`, `oil_reference.py` (a copy of the oil prototype); `scripts/world_check.py` (the measurement driver, section 3); profiles `scripts/profiles/b8-world-s{1..5}.json`. Interfaces, flags and `--check` semantics of every tool are in section 1.9.

**Placement table.** `paths.py` is the one place that knows file locations; it recognises the lead modeller's working tree (`spec_work/` and `research/`, not published) and the repo layout (`<repo>/scripts/world`, or any tree named by the environment variable `WORLD_ROOT`), and no other script hard-wires a path. Stage 1 copies the files as below; **`python3 -B -I scripts/world/world_ref.py selftest` and `goldens --check`, run in the repo layout, are the acceptance check of this table** (they were run in a scratch copy built by `scripts/world/make_layout.py` DEST` and reproduce every golden: max difference 0). `GD` = `crates/econsim-core/src/world/data/golden/`.

| File | Lead modeller's tree | Repo |
|---|---|---|
| 18 Python tools (list above) | `spec_work/ref/` | `scripts/world/` |
| P\* table | `spec_work/pstar_blocs.json` | `scripts/world/pstar.json` |
| target registry (selftest compares the bands) | `spec_work/targets.json` | `scripts/world/targets.json` |
| oil prototype | `research/oil/scripts/oil_market_model.py` | `scripts/world/oil_reference.py` |
| research parameters | `research/{blocs,trade,oil,finance}/params.json` | `data/world/research/<x>/params.json` |
| 5 bloc CSVs (`bloc_spillover_coeffs_2024`, `bloc_trade_weights_2024`, `discretisation_check`, `own_shock_irfs_gpm`, `state_space_eigs`) | `spec_work/data/` | `data/world/research/blocs/data/` |
| 3 oil CSVs (`eia_intl_annual_liquids_1990_2025`, `steo_monthly_tidy`, `oil_model_replay_2026`) | `research/oil/data/` | `data/world/research/oil/data/` |
| golden: hold-12 of all 12 blocs, I&N group statistics, gate values (`W1-*`) | `spec_work/golden_stage1.json` | `GD` `stage1.json` |
| golden: replay of the I&N path alone, no extra shock, 60 months, with the reference `out` | `spec_work/golden_inputs_in2019_clean.jsonl` | `GD` `inputs_in2019_clean.jsonl` |
| golden: replay with BlocPolicy EA at t0 = 6, BlocDemand CN, Dollar +5% at 24, oil +5 from 12, ius x 3 with the M-F bound on | `spec_work/golden_inputs_stress.jsonl` | `GD` `inputs_stress.jsonl` |
| golden: unit-shock IRFs, 61 months, shocks demand / cost_push / policy / risk_premium, blocs EA, JP, UK, MX (W1-06) | `spec_work/golden_irf_unit.json` | `GD` `irf_unit.json` |
| golden: stacked perfect-foresight path of EA with theta 0, ius = 0.95^t, 60 months (W1-09) | `spec_work/golden_pf_ar1_EA.json` | `GD` `pf_ar1_EA.json` |
| golden: period-0 responses for K = 480 and K = 960, 12 blocs x 4 equations x persistence {0.99, 1.0} (W1-32) | `spec_work/golden_kref.json` | `GD` `kref.json` |
| golden: trade hand values, full precision, h = 0..60 (stage 2) | `spec_work/golden_trade_hand.json` | `GD` `trade_hand.json` |
| golden: oil IRFs and episodes (stage 3, written by `oil_reference.py`) | | `GD` `oil_golden.json` |
| generated and embedded: `world_params.json`, `bloc_solution.json` | | `crates/econsim-core/src/world/data/` |

Edits to existing files are limited to the hooks of section 1.4. Every hook is a guarded call; when `w.world` is `None` (or the relevant link is off) the legacy expression runs unchanged.

### 1.2 State

```rust
pub struct WorldBlock {                 // World gets: pub world: Option<Box<WorldBlock>>   (None in World::empty)
    pub params: Arc<WorldParams>,       // immutable tables (cheap Clone)
    pub blocs: [BlocState; 12],         // order EA, CN, JP, UK, CA, MX, IN, KR, BR, AU, OPEC+, RoW
    pub us: UsHist,                     // ius history (4), y_US history, okun gap, last inputs
    pub trade: Option<TradeState>,      // stage 2
    pub oil: Option<OilState>,          // stage 3 (market A and, when a user oil shock is live, the shadow copy U)
    pub dollar: Option<DollarState>,    // stage 4
    pub fin: Option<FinState>,          // stage 4
    pub pos: Option<PositionsState>,    // stage 4
    pub bases: Option<Bases>,           // ln S_j,0, ln P_j,0, PT_0, fx_0: captured at the first world step of the reported run (section 1.6)
    pub out: WorldOut,                  // this month's values read by other modules (Copy-able small struct)
    pub diag: WorldMetrics,             // filled during the month, copied into Metrics by metrics::record
}
pub struct BlocState { s: [f64; 33], hist: BlocHist /* y, pi, i, z, mrr last 14 months, plus z_exo, ln_p, ln_s */, g: f64, u_rp: f64, ... }
```

`WorldBlock: Clone + Debug` (derive). `compare()` clones the whole world; the `Arc` is shared.

### 1.3 Tick order and lags

```
begin_month
exog::step                 ... legacy oil draws still taken; exog.oil_price guarded (hook H3)
world::after_exog          NEW (stage 3, 4): oil market clears (reads bloc gaps of month t-1); policy surprise injected into f_t
monetary::set_policy
monetary::update_curve     (hook H5: + official-demand term from the state of month t-1)
monetary::exchange_rate    runs only if !world::dollar_active(w)   (hook H2; unchanged position: right after the curve)
world::step                NEW: US inputs -> dollar phase A (premium, addend, Frisk, zeta) -> blocs -> dollar phase B (bilateral rates, broad index, w.row.fx) -> trade prices/tariffs -> finance flows   (rev 7: the stage-1 bases are captured at the first reported step; the trade and dollar bases at the end of the last burn-in month; no ABM state is overwritten)
banking::set_rates ... goods::clear (hooks H6-H9) ... banking::service, fiscal::collect_and_finance, monetary::markets (hook H10)
firms::close_books, consumption::record_capital_income, world::settle   (in step_timed this is the existing `firms.close` closure; settle = bilateral decomposition of this month's realised flows, positions, identities)
metrics::record            (hook H11: copies WorldBlock.diag into Metrics.world)
ledger::check_consistency  (debug) + world::check_identities (debug)
```

| Lag | Why |
|---|---|
| Oil clearing in `after_exog` uses bloc gaps of month `t-1` (`ylag` smoothing, section 2.4) | The bloc step needs the oil price of month `t`; one-month lag breaks the simultaneity. |
| Blocs use US `y` of month `t-1` and the ABM's expected policy path (from `update_curve` of month `t`, which already includes this month's policy rate) | `y_US` for month `t` is not known before `goods::clear`. |
| Cross-bloc inputs (`F`, `P`) use bloc outputs of month `t-1` | Same-month cross terms would need an iteration; the offline solution is per bloc. |
| Official-demand term in `update_curve` uses the official tranche of month `t-1`; flows of month `t` are executed in `world::step` after the curve | The curve is priced before the flows. The trend GDP the block reads in `world::step` of month `t` is the one `plan_spending` left in month `t-1` (it runs after `world::step`). |
| Origin import prices and tariffs for `goods::clear` of month `t` use the dollar of month `t` (computed in `world::step` in dollar mode; by `exchange_rate`, which runs just before `world::step`, otherwise) and the tradables price of month `t-1` | `PT_us` is only known after clearing. |
| Positions use flows realised in month `t` | `settle` runs after the clearing. |
| `fx_base` of the ABM and `trade_import_z` | **not overwritten** (S2-R1, F1, F5): both continue from the burn-in at the first reported month, so the terms ratio is continuous. (Earlier: overwritten at the first reported world step when `trade_active` or `dollar_active`.) In dollar mode `exchange_rate` never runs, so it never captures them. |
| World bases (`ln S_j,0`, `ln P_j,0`, `PT_0`, `p_world,0`, `fx_0`) | captured at the first world step for which `reported(w)` holds (D36: after `build_world`'s tick reset, or tick 0 for a world without burn-in), (the stage-1 bases; the trade bases are captured at the end of the last burn-in month, next row). |
| Trade bases (section 2.5) | captured at the end of the last burn-in month (D36, S2-R1) from the ABM's own values: `fx_0`, `p_world,0`, `PT_0`, `base_s`, `imp_level`, `kappa_s`, `z_base`, `world_f0`, volume bases. A world without burn-in captures them at its first step. |
